The norm-one stabilizer has reductive identity component SO(v-perp). For N>=3 rotations lie in the derived subgroup and act nontrivially. In N<=2 the acting group is a torus. Inspection: Exact retained and versioned primary theorem inspected, and applicability checked by the displayed open orbit and stabilizer argument. | The dimension cutoff and fixed-witness existence over C follow from Long. Wick expansion factors out critical action and Hessian determinant, evaluates the higher interaction formally, kills total derivatives and is invariant under formal coordinate changes independently of integral convergence. The selected g solves the stationary equation for tP-X^T X/2. Translation X=g+Y removes its linear part; L=(I-t Hess P(g))^(-1/2) normalizes the quadratic part. Since g=O(t), L=I+O(t), each fixed t coefficient is a finite Wick computation. The repository proof supplies the exact parameter-dependent identity and every original amplitude. The radical is the intersection of prime ideals; finite-type field algebras satisfy Hilbert Nullstellensatz and are Noetherian; in a Noetherian ring an ideal contained in another ideal's radical has some power contained in that ideal. Assume the retained strong field Gamma tail theorem. Reducing a Q-algebra modulo every prime and passing to the fraction field puts every coefficient of a tail-vanishing polynomial in every prime, hence in the nilradical. There are finitely many coefficients; if their nilpotence exponents are e_i, the coefficient ideal has power 1+sum(e_i-1) equal to zero by the local written pigeonhole proof. This part needs no Noetherian hypothesis and does not apply the Noetherian statement of tag00IM to an arbitrary algebra. For a fixed finite coefficient polynomial family, the field zero-locus theorem and Nullstellensatz give radical(I_infinity)=m; Noetherianity selects some finite prefix, and tag00IM gives some coefficient-ideal power in it. This is exactly the finite-colength deduction. Inspection: No exact radical/Noetherian external mapping was previously attached to these Gaussian coefficient corollaries. | **Full polynomial symplectic frames admit formal lifts by algebraic Fedosov classification** (ANT-DIX-AFFINE-FEDOSOV-FULL-FRAME-LIFT). DIXMIER-FORMAL-LIFTING, DIX-QFL1, DIX-QFL1-S4. [Fedosov quantization in algebraic context](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0309290v4) :: Theorem1.8, arXiv:math/0309290v4 pp10-11; Definition1.1 p6; Definition1.3 p7 and Remark1.6 p9; fixed-marking interpretation in Section2.4 p16 and Lemma3.4 pp22-23; period-map proof Section4 pp30-32. For a smooth admissible symplectic scheme over a characteristic-zero field, the period map from isomorphism classes of quantizations into H2_dR[[h]] is injective. Affine quantizations correspond to flat h-complete algebras with specified classical reduction. Consequently all quantizations of symplectic affine space are isomorphic with the fixed classical marking, since its H2_dR vanishes. This is an inferred corollary, not an endomorphism theorem stated verbatim in BK. Let A=K[x_1,...,x_(2n)] and let f satisfy {f_i,f_j}=J_ij for constant nondegenerate J. The inverse Jacobian is polynomial, so the derivations E_i defined by E_i(f_j)=delta_ij are polynomial and commute: their brackets vanish on all f_j and the df_j are an A-basis. Define star_f=m exp((h/2) sum_ij J_ij E_i tensor E_j). Commutativity of the E_i gives associativity, and its underlying module A[[h]] is flat and h-complete. It has the same first-order Poisson tensor as the original Moyal product, while [f_i,f_j]_(star_f)=h J_ij exactly. On X=Spec A, H1(X,O_X)=H2(X,O_X)=0 gives admissibility and the polynomial Euler homotopy gives H2_dR(X)=0. Period injectivity therefore identifies star_f with the original Moyal quantization. The isomorphism T respects the fixed reduction A and hence T=id modulo h. Its images T(f_i) give the desired polynomial-coefficient formal Weyl frame. No inverse polynomial map f^-1, spatial completion or finite etaleness hypothesis is used. These marked objects establish identity modulo h, rather than an arbitrary classical symplectic automorphism. [The Stacks Project](https://stacks.math.columbia.edu) :: Lemmas58.31.5 and58.31.6, tags0EYG and0EYH; Lemmas49.8.2--3, tags0BW2--3; Lemmas49.12.4 and49.12.6, tags0BWH and0BWJ. A finite etale cover tamely ramified along a regular Cartier divisor has normalization etale locally given by u^e=t with e invertible. The finite trace-dual is the relative dualizing module with trace element corresponding to1; for a quasi-finite complete intersection that trace element is represented by the Jacobian determinant. Thus the local inverse different in the tame model is (e*u^(e-1))^-1 times the normal local ring. Use the full finite normal order S over B=C[P,Q]. For a target prime curve C, shrink around its generic point so C is regular and remove the other branch components and the finitely many residual non-etale/singular points. The finite cover on its complement is etale; characteristic zero makes every relevant ramification index invertible and residue extension separable. Stacks0EYG--0EYH supplies t=u^e after etale base change, which preserves the normalized divisor valuation and detects the local fractional lattice. The Jacobian is e*u^(e-1), so ordinary trace-dual membership is exactly v_E(a)>=1-e_E. For a regular target derivation transverse to C, D(u)=D(t)/(e*u^(e-1)) has order1-e. If f=u^-q*a with q>0 and a a unit, its leading differentiated term is -(q/e)*a*D(t)*u^(-q-e); terms from differentiating a have strictly larger order and q is nonzero in characteristic zero. Therefore v_E(Df)=-q-e, below the codifferent threshold. A regular f instead gives v_E(Df)>=1-e. [factorial/image-rigidity/HIGHER_GAP_ADJOINT_RANK.tex](factorial/image-rigidity/HIGHER_GAP_ADJOINT_RANK.tex) :: thm:aux Eq(3)–(6), raising recurrence and exponential generating function; [factorial/image-rigidity/PHYSICAL_PRIME_RAISING_CALCULUS.tex](factorial/image-rigidity/PHYSICAL_PRIME_RAISING_CALCULUS.tex) :: lem:content proof, fixed-shape B_{n,gamma,q}=Pi_n(gamma+q-1-theta+z)1 and its fixed-shape generating series | classical_method. [Explicit Formulas and Combinatorial Identities for Generalized Stirling Numbers](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00009-011-0169-x) :: Author manuscript p2 Eq(1.1), recurrence(1.3), generating function(1.4); Theorem2.3 and proof pp5–6. The Hsu–Shiue connection coefficients S(n,k;alpha,beta,r) satisfy S(n+1,k)=S(n,k-1)+(k*beta-n*alpha+r)S(n,k), and their vertical exponential generating function is (1+alpha*t)^(r/alpha)*(((1+alpha*t)^(beta/alpha)-1)/beta)^k/k! for alpha*beta!=0. Parameters may be real or complex. Put c=gamma+d-1, alpha=-d, beta=-1, r=c. Then exactly B_{n,gamma,d}(z)=sum_{k=0}^n S(n,k;-d,-1,c)z^k. Summing the cited recurrence gives B_(n+1)=(z+dn+c)B_n-zB_n', with B_0=1. Summing the vertical generating functions gives precisely (1-dt)^(-c/d)*exp(z*(1-(1-dt)^(1/d))), including the local normalization. Thus the recurrence and named polynomial family are existing generalized-Stirling/Sheffer structure. In the physical diagonal gamma=n the parameter r=n+d-1 changes with n; fixed-parameter Sheffer statements cannot be applied across that diagonal without the separate inversion step. It does not yield digit content, integral operator bounds, or all-index rank exclusion. |
